{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_vrtx_01CSD5dJgRwW1HQq8T1GiWb3","type":"tool_result","content":"version = \"1.0\"\nreward_type = \"binary\"\n\n[task]\nname = \"scale-ai/space_wizards__space_station_14_36889_mm\"\n\n[metadata]\ntask_id = \"space_wizards__space_station_14_36889_mm\"\ntitle = \"Doors on the test bench misbehave\"\nrepository_name = \"space-wizards/space-station-14\"\nrepo_url = \"https://github.com/space-wizards/space-station-14\"\nissue_number = 36889\npr_number = 46007\nbase_commit = \"58dae1ce4a0806fa3b928834789b2b508b47b0f3\"\nfix_commit = \"763186613f196824f010bba663d353e4cce8f3ea\"\ntask_type = \"temporal_interaction\"\nlanguage = \"\"\nframework = \"Space Station 14\"\ncross_file = true\nvisual_essentiality = \"required\"\ndifficulty = \"hard\"\ndifficulty_explanation = \"\"\"\nThe clip is a firelock test bench recorded from the real client: six one-tile lanes, each firelock between a small room and a hazard (an exposed lattice open to space, dense gas, fire, fire behind a glass firelock, gas behind an edge firelock, and one firelock that starts open). The instruction names the doors only by type ('doors'), never the kind (firelock, glass firelock, edge firelock), the hazard, the lane or the direction, and it states no defect in words or how many there are. It names only the aspects that go wrong (the warning lights, the way a door moves, what ends up on each side of a shut door), adds one clip-only pointer ('some of them are already there in the first frame and never change', which covers the static lanes: the space lane's warning colour, the glass firelock with no warning, and the gas past the edge firelock's panel, without saying which lane, door or hazard), gives general expected rules (a shut door keeps each side apart and its warning says what kind of danger it holds back, the warning only shows while shut, a door shuts smoothly as a whole), and asks for every mismatch found in the clip to be fixed. Each Expected rule is enforced by f2p tests: 'keeps each side apart' -> ClosedFirelock_HoldsBackGasReleasedAfterClosing and EdgeFirelock_SealsAlongItsDoorEdge (the edge door's seal must sit on the edge its panel is drawn on, otherwise the hazard fills the door's own tile up to the room side of the panel, which the gas overlay shows); 'its warning says what kind of danger' -> WarningLights_SpaceBreachIsAPressureWarning and WarningLights_GlassFirelockShowsWarning; 'the warning only shows while shut' -> WarningLights_HideWhenDoorLeavesClosed; 'shuts smoothly, as a whole' -> ClosingAnimation_PlaysThroughBeforeTheDoorShuts ('smoothly': the panel plays through its closing frames instead of jumping shut in one frame; 'as a whole': the panel moves in step with its running lights instead of finishing while they are still sliding). Each of the six defects sits in a different place and most of them only reveal themselves in the clip:\n\n1. Warning lights ignore the door state (the upstream issue): the pressure/temperature layers are driven by a declarative GenericVisualizer that only reads the two warning booleans, so an opened or re-closing firelock keeps its brackets lit (covered by the 'warning only shows while shut' rule). The fix moves the layers into the client FirelockSystem's appearance handler (Closed/Welded/Denying only) and drops the visualizer.\n2. The closing animation is cut short: the frame timings of the standard firelock sprite's closing state (RSI meta.json) are ten times too short, so the panel jumps from open to shut in one frame and its green running lights then slide over an already-shut door (the opening in the same clip slides normally, and the opening state in the same meta.json is the reference). The prototype's animation times and the client door code are both correct, so only frame-stepping the clip points at it.\n3. The lattice/space lane shows the temperature warning instead of the pressure one: the firelock's temperature-difference threshold default was lowered so that space's ~2.7 K against a room at 293 K counts as a temperature hazard. The over-pressure lane next to it shows the pressure colour, the fire lanes the temperature colour.\n4. The glass firelock shows no warning at all while holding back fire (it still glows): its prototype overrides the sprite layer list and leaves out the two warning layers, so the old visualizer only reserves blank layers. Needs prototype-inheritance knowledge.\n5. The edge firelock seals the wrong edge of its tile: its airBlockedDirection was flipped, so gas fills the door's own tile past the hazard strip (its fixture and sprite sit on the other edge). Only the gas overlay in the clip shows it.\n6. A firelock shut during the round does not seal: AirtightSystem.SetAirblocked only re-validates the atmos tile when a door opens (a two-line conditional with no comment), so a door that closes later never blocks air and the gas released behind it flows straight through. Doors that were shut by monstermos or at map load are unaffected, which is why the other lanes hold.\n\nWithout the clip the instruction does not say which kind of firelock, which hazard, which animation or which direction is wrong; with it, every defect except the open door's lights has a correct counterpart in another lane, and that one is covered by an expected rule. The fix touches five files: client visuals, shared component data, server atmos, three prototypes in one YAML file, and one sprite's frame metadata.\n\"\"\"\nfiles_edited = [\n Resets every path test.patch touches to the immutable harbor_base tag, applies\n# the hidden tests, runs ONLY the configured f2p + p2p tests with the repository's own runner into a\n# JUnit report, and lets verifier.py turn that report into the reward. Nothing here reads the\n# candidate's source to decide anything.\nset -uo pipefail\n\n# ---- task-specific values, filled by the task builder ----------------------------------------\n# run_tests runs the configured tests with the repository's own runner and writes JUnit XML to\n# $JUNIT_OUT. The test ids (f2p + p2p from /tests/config.json) are in $TEST_IDS_FILE, one per line.\n# Write plain shell commands here; no quoting of the whole block is needed.\nrun_tests() {\n  cd /app\n\n  # Build a VSTest --filter expression that matches exactly the configured test ids (by fully\n  # qualified name), OR'd together.\n  FILTER=\"\"\n  while IFS= read -r id; do\n    [ -z \"$id\" ] && continue\n    clause=\"FullyQualifiedName=${id}\"\n    if [ -z \"$FILTER\" ]; then\n      FILTER=\"$clause\"\n    else\n      FILTER=\"${FILTER}|${clause}\"\n    fi\n  done < \"$TEST_IDS_FILE\"\n\n  WORKDIR=\"$(mktemp -d)\"\n  NUNIT_XML_DIR=\"$WORKDIR/nunit-xml\"\n  mkdir -p \"$NUNIT_XML_DIR\"\n\n  # Rebuild (offline: the image already restored every NuGet package, and a source-only\n  # patch does not change what needs restoring). Only the integration test project and what it\n  # references (Content.Client, Content.Server, Content.Shared, Content.Tests and the engine)\n  # are built -- the rest of the solution (benchmarks, map renderer, packaging, linters, tools)\n  # plays no part in the tests. -m lets MSBuild use multiple processes; this stays on top of\n  # the image's own cached build rather than a from-scratch compile.\n  dotnet build Content.IntegrationTests/Content.IntegrationTests.csproj --configuration DebugOpt --no-restore /m\n\n  # NUnit.NumberOfTestWorkers=1 overrides the assembly's [LevelOfParallelism(2)] and runs the\n  # selected tests one at a time on a single dedicated worker thread, instead of on two\n  # concurrent worker threads (NumberOfTestWorkers=0, which drops NUnit's threaded dispatcher\n  # entirely and runs tests directly on the calling thread, turned out to be incompatible with\n  # this engine's GameTest fixtures and produced a wrongly-attributed report -- a result from one\n  # test landing under the other test's name -- so a single *worker thread* is used instead).\n  # With only a couple of tests selected by filter (instead of the full suite), two GameTest-\n  # derived tests can each spin up their own Robust server/client pair at the same time; those\n  # pairs race on the engine's prototype-loading path (PrototypeManager.InstantiateKinds ->\n  # KindData.Freeze) and the second server to start can intermittently hit an\n  # ArgumentNullException while building its prototype dictionary. That's a pre-existing\n  # concurrency bug in the engine's test-pool startup, not something either test or the fix\n  # touches, so it's worked around here by serializing execution onto one worker thread.\n  dotnet test --no-build --configuration DebugOpt Content.IntegrationTests/Content.IntegrationTests.csproj \\\n      --filter \"$FILTER\" \\\n      --logger \"console;verbosity=normal\" \\\n      -- NUnit.ConsoleOut=0 NUnit.MapWarningTo=Failed \\\n         NUnit.NumberOfTestWorkers=1 \\\n         NUnit.TestOutputXml=\"$(basename \"$NUNIT_XML_DIR\")\" \\\n         NUnit.WorkDirectory=\"$WORKDIR\" \\\n      || true   # test failures must not abort the script; we still need to emit JUnit XML\n\n  # Convert whatever NUnit3-format XML files were produced into one JUnit-style report.\n  python3 - \"$NUNIT_XML_DIR\" \"$JUNIT_OUT\" \"$FILTER\" <<'PY'\nimport sys, glob, os\nimport xml.etree.ElementTree as ET\n\nnunit_dir, out_path, filt = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3]\n\ncases = []  # (classname, name, status, message)\nfor path in glob.glob(os.path.join(nunit_dir, \"*.xml\")):\n    try:\n        root = ET.parse(path).getroot()\n    except ET.ParseError:\n        continue\n    for tc in root.iter(\"test-case\"):\n        classname = tc.get(\"classname\") or \"\"\n        name = tc.get(\"methodname\") or tc.get(\"name\") or \"\"\n        fullname = tc.get(\"fullname\") or f\"{classname}.{name}\"\n        result = (tc.get(\"result\") or \"\").lower()\n        msg = \"\"\n        failure = tc.find(\"failure\")\n        if failure is not None:\n            m = failure.find(\"message\")\n            st = failure.find(\"stack-trace\")\n            msg = ((m.text or \"\") if m is not None else \"\") + \"\\n\" + ((st.text or \"\") if st is not None else \"\")\n        if result in (\"passed\",):\n            status = \"pass\"\n        elif result in (\"skipped\", \"ignored\", \"explicit\"):\n            status = \"skip\"\n        else:\n            status = \"fail\"\n        cases.append((classname, name, fullname, status, msg))\n\nsuite = ET.Element(\"testsuite\", {\"name\": \"Content.IntegrationTests\", \"tests\": str(len(cases))})\nfor classname, name, fullname, status, msg in cases:\n    # classname left empty and the full \"Namespace.Class.Method\" dotted id put in \"name\" so that\n    # verifier.py's \"{classname} {name}\".strip() key equals config.json's f2p/p2p ids exactly.\n    tc_el = ET.SubElement(suite, \"testcase\", {\"classname\": \"\", \"name\": fullname})\n    if status == \"fail\":\n        f_el = ET.SubElement(tc_el, \"failure\", {\"message\": (msg or \"failed\")[:500]})\n        f_el.text = msg[:4000]\n    elif status == \"skip\":\n        ET.SubElement(tc_el, \"skipped\")\n\nET.ElementTree(suite).write(out_path, encoding=\"unicode\", xml_declaration=True)\nprint(f\"wrote {len(cases)} test-case results to {out_path}\")\nPY\n}\n# Directories holding the repo's test plumbing (configs, helpers) that must also be restored so a\n# candidate cannot replace the harness. Space-separated, relative to /app; '' if none.\nPROTECTED_DIRS='Content.IntegrationTests/Fixtures'\n# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nmkdir -p /logs/verifier\nSTDOUT_LOG=/logs/verifier/test-stdout.txt\nSTDERR_LOG=/logs/verifier/test-stderr.txt\nexport JUNIT_OUT=/logs/verifier/junit.xml\nexport TEST_IDS_FILE=/logs/verifier/test_ids.txt\n: > \"$STDOUT_LOG\"; : > \"$STDERR_LOG\"\nrm -f \"$JUNIT_OUT\" /logs/verifier/reward.txt /logs/verifier/reward.json\n\nensure_reward() { [ -f /logs/verifier/reward.txt ] || echo 0 > /logs/verifier/reward.txt; }\ntrap ensure_reward EXIT\n\ncd /app || { echo \"no /app\" | tee -a \"$STDERR_LOG\"; exit 1; }\n\n# Capture the agent's submission BEFORE anything below mutates /app -- this is the last\n# moment the tree is base + agent work; the restore loop and test.patch come next.\n#\n# A THROWAWAY INDEX, never /app/.git/index: `git add` against the real one would leave paths\n# staged and change how the `git checkout harbor_base --` and `git clean` below behave.\n#\n# TRACKED EDITS + A SOURCE WHITELIST, not `add -A` with a blacklist. The image builds the .NET\n# solution in-tree AFTER the harbor_base commit (environment/Dockerfile), so build output sits in\n# the working tree as untracked files. A bare `add -A` risks sweeping up generated output along\n# with the agent's work. Inverting it -- stage tracked modifications, then add only untracked\n# files that LOOK like source -- keeps the agent's work and drops generated output by default.\n_SUB=/logs/verifier/submission.patch\n_SUBIDX=/tmp/.submission_index\n_SUBSRC=('*.c' '*.cc' '*.cpp' '*.cxx' '*.h' '*.hh' '*.hpp' '*.hxx' '*.inl' '*.m' '*.mm'\n         '*.py' '*.rb' '*.go' '*.rs' '*.java' '*.js' '*.ts' '*.tsx' '*.cs' '*.kt' '*.swift')\nrm -f \"$_SUBIDX\"\n# Seed the throwaway index from a COPY of the real one (harbor_base plus anything the agent\n# staged): it carries git's cached file stats, so `add -u` below only re-hashes files that\n# actually changed. A bare `read-tree harbor_base` has no stats and makes `add -u` re-hash every\n# tracked file in this large repo (minutes per run). The real index itself is never written.\nif ! cp /app/.git/index \"$_SUBIDX\" 2>>\"$STDERR_LOG\"; then\n  GIT_INDEX_FILE=\"$_SUBIDX\" git -C /app read-tree harbor_base 2>>\"$STDERR_LOG\" || true\nfi\nGIT_INDEX_FILE=\"$_SUBIDX\" git -C /app add -u 2>>\"$STDERR_LOG\" || true\n# Enumerate before adding: `git add` aborts and stages NOTHING if any one pathspec matches\n# no file, which silently drops the agent's new files on most tasks.\n# Generated-output dirs are dropped from the UNTRACKED scan only. A project that keeps real\n# source under lib/ is unaffected: those files are tracked at harbor_base, so `add -u` above\n# already has them. This drops only files CREATED under a build dir during the run -- which,\n# left in, break replay: they are recorded as new-file hunks and a fresh container from the\n# same image already has them, so `git apply` fails with \"already exists in working directory\".\nmapfile -t _SUBNEW < <(git -C /app ls-files --others --exclude-standard \\\n  -- \"${_SUBSRC[@]}\" ':(exclude)lib/' ':(exclude)build/' ':(exclude).deps/' 2>/dev/null)\nif [ \"${#_SUBNEW[@]}\" -gt 0 ]; then\n  GIT_INDEX_FILE=\"$_SUBIDX\" git -C /app add -- \"${_SUBNEW[@]}\" 2>>\"$STDERR_LOG\" || true\nfi\nGIT_INDEX_FILE=\"$_SUBIDX\" git -C /app diff --binary --cached harbor_base \\\n  > \"$_SUB\" 2>>\"$STDERR_LOG\" || true\nrm -f \"$_SUBIDX\"\n\n# WHAT THE WHITELIST DROPPED, listed rather than silently discarded. The split above captures\n# every change to a TRACKED file (any extension, deletions included) but only NEW files that\n# look like source -- so an agent adding config.yml, a .cmake module or an extensionless script\n# loses it, and the patch still looks clean. That is the failure this manifest exists to expose.\n#\n# NEWEST FIRST, which is the useful ordering and not a cosmetic one: build output and image\n# assets are created before the agent phase starts, so anything the agent made sorts to the top.\n# Build artifacts are excluded by the same rules as the capture, to keep the list readable.\n#\n# If something here belongs in the submission, widen _SUBSRC. Once a judge sub-agent has written\n# verdict.json there, `mm_run.sh <task> rescore` folds the verdict into the reward.\n# The `|| echo \"\"` is load-bearing: the judge must never be able to fail the run.\nRUBRIC=\"\"\nif [ -f /tests/rubrics.json ]; then\n  RUBRIC=$(python3 /tests/judge.py --rubrics /tests/rubrics.json --repo /app \\\n             2>/logs/verifier/judge.log || echo \"\")\n  echo \"Rubric verdict: ${RUBRIC:-pending (judge request written; rescore after grading)}\"\nfi\n\npython3 /tests/verifier.py --rubric \"${RUBRIC:-}\" 2>>\"$STDERR_LOG\" | tee -a \"$STDOUT_LOG\"\n\necho \"reward=$(cat /logs/verifier/reward.txt 2>/dev/null)\" | tee -a \"$STDOUT_LOG\"\n---\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Turn the JUnit report into the task reward.\n\nThe reward is binary: 1.0 only when every configured f2p and p2p test is found exactly once in the\nreport and passed; otherwise 0.0. f2p_passed / f2p_total and p2p_passed / p2p_total record how close\na run came. The verifier reads test outcomes only, never the candidate's source.\n\"\"\"\nimport html\nimport json\nimport os\nimport xml.etree.ElementTree as ET\n\nimport atexit, json as _cj, os as _co, sys as _cs\n\n_CANON_JSON = \"/logs/verifier/reward.json\"\n_CANON_TXT = \"/logs/verifier/reward.txt\"\n_canon = {}\n\n\ndef canon_reward(f2p_passed, f2p_total, p2p_passed, p2p_total, rubric):\n    \"\"\"THE reward policy for the whole corpus. BINARY -- there is no partial credit.\n\n    tests_passed is 1.0 only when every f2p test passes AND every p2p test still passes. A\n    strict subset of f2p passing is not partial progress worth scoring: the task is unsolved,\n    so it is 0.0.\n\n    reward is the same value, zeroed when the rubric judge failed a criterion. A \"pass\" or a\n    \"not graded\" verdict never lowers the score -- the judge must never be able to penalise a\n    run it could not grade.\n    \"\"\"\n    f, t = int(f2p_passed), int(f2p_total)\n    p, q = int(p2p_passed), int(p2p_total)\n    solved = bool(t) and f == t and p == q\n    tests_passed = 1.0 if solved else 0.0\n    _canon.clear()\n    _canon.update({\n        \"reward\": 0.0 if rubric == \"fail\" else tests_passed,\n        \"tests_passed\": tests_passed,\n        \"f2p_passed\": f, \"f2p_total\": t,\n        \"p2p_passed\": p, \"p2p_total\": q,\n        \"rubric_passed\": {\"pass\": 1, \"fail\": 0}.get(rubric, -1),\n    })\n    return _canon[\"reward\"]\n\n\n@atexit.register\ndef _canon_flush():\n    if not _canon:\n        return\n    try:\n        _co.makedirs(_co.path.dirname(_CANON_JSON), exist_ok=True)\n        with open(_CANON_JSON, \"w\") as _h:\n            _cj.dump(_canon, _h, indent=2)\n        # reward.txt too: harbor prefers reward.json, but the sandbox teardown script and the\n        # lighthouse UI read reward.txt. Writing both from one dict means they cannot disagree.\n        with open(_CANON_TXT, \"w\") as _h:\n            _h.write(str(_canon[\"reward\"]))\n    except Exception as _exc:\n        print(\"WARNING: could not write %s: %s\" % (_CANON_JSON, _exc), file=_cs.stderr)\n\n\ndef rubric_verdict(argv):\n    \"\"\"The verdict test.sh hands in as `--rubric pass|fail|\"\"`.\n\n    Anything that is not an explicit pass/fail -- including the empty string test.sh passes\n    when judge.py wrote no verdict -- means \"not graded\", which must never penalise the run.\n    \"\"\"\n    if \"--rubric\" not in argv:\n        return None\n    i = argv.index(\"--rubric\")\n    value = argv[i + 1].strip().lower() if i + 1 < len(argv) else \"\"\n    return value if value in (\"pass\", \"fail\") else None\n\n\nCONF = json.load(open(\"/tests/config.json\"))\nF2P, P2P = CONF[\"f2p\"], CONF[\"p2p\"]\nREPORT = os.environ.get(\"JUNIT_OUT\", \"/logs/verifier/junit.xml\")\nOUT_DIR = \"/logs/verifier\"\n\n\ndef load_junit(path):\n    \"\"\"Every reported test, keyed by \"classname name\" and by name; a key seen twice with different\n    outcomes counts as failed.\"\"\"\n    results = {}\n    if not os.path.exists(path):\n        return results\n    try:\n        root = ET.parse(path).getroot()\n    except ET.ParseError:\n        return results\n    for tc in root.iter(\"testcase\"):\n        tags = {c.tag for c in tc}\n        outcome = \"skip\" if \"skipped\" in tags else (\"fail\" if tags & {\"failure\", \"error\"} else \"pass\")\n        full = f\"{tc.get('classname', '')} {tc.get('name', '')}\".strip()\n        for key in {full, tc.get(\"name\", \"\")}:\n            if key in results and results[key] != outcome:\n                results[key] = \"fail\"\n            else:\n                results[key] = outcome\n    return results","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_vrtx_012Hw3Um3PRqxrAEBr68ACrC","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}
